Two-body Baryonic B u , d , s B_{u,d,s} and B c B_{c} to Charmless Final State Decays
Chun-Khiang Chua

Abstract
We study the rates and direct CP violations of two-body baryonic B ¯ u , d , s → 𝐁 ​ 𝐁 ¯ ′ \bar{B}_{u,d,s}\to{{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}}^{\prime} and B c − → 𝐁 ​ 𝐁 ¯ ′ B^{-}_{c}\to{{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}}^{\prime} decays, where the final state baryons include low-lying octet and decuplet baryons. We incorporate topological amplitude formalism and the factorization approach. Asymptotic relations at large m b m_{b} are used to simplify decay amplitudes. Using the most up-to-date data on B ¯ 0 → p ​ p ¯ \bar{B}^{0}\to p\bar{p} and B − → Λ ​ p ¯ B^{-}\to\Lambda\bar{p} decay rates as inputs, rates and direct CP violations of B ¯ u , d , s → 𝐁 ​ 𝐁 ¯ ′ \bar{B}_{u,d,s}\to{{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}}^{\prime} decays are revised and predicted. It is interesting that the results on rates satisfy all existing experimental bounds and some are close to the bounds. Factorization diagrams contribute to penguin-exchange, exchange, annihilation and penguin-annihilation amplitudes. Although the resulting penguin-exchange factorization amplitudes are sizable, the rest suffer from severe chiral suppression and are sensitive to non-factorizable contributions. As the B ¯ s → p ​ p ¯ \bar{B}_{s}\to p\bar{p} decay is governed by exchange and penguin annihilation amplitudes, the rate predicted in factorization calculation is very rare, but it can be enhanced by including non-factorizable contributions. The case where the rate is enhanced to saturate the present experimental bound through the enhancement on exchange or penguin-annihilation amplitudes is discussed. As annihilation modes B c − → 𝐁 ​ 𝐁 ¯ ′ B^{-}_{c}\to{{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}}^{\prime} decays from factorization calculation are found to be very rare, but they can be enhanced by including non-factorizable contributions as well. Small direct CP violations of pure penguin modes in Δ ​ S = − 1 \Delta S=-1 B ¯ u , d , s → 𝐁 ​ 𝐁 ¯ ′ \bar{B}_{u,d,s}\to{{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}}^{\prime} decays are robust predictions of the SM, while vanishing direct CP violations of exchange modes in B ¯ u , d , s → 𝐁 ​ 𝐁 ¯ ′ \bar{B}_{u,d,s}\to{{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}}^{\prime} decays and in all B c − → 𝐁 ​ 𝐁 ¯ ′ B^{-}_{c}\to{{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}}^{\prime} decay modes are null tests of the SM.



pacs
11.30.Hv, 13.25.Hw, 14.40.Nd

I
Introduction

Two body baryonic
B
decays with octet and decuplet baryons have been searched experimentally for some time.
The present situation is summarized in Table
1

LHCb:2016nbc
;
Belle:2007gob
;
Belle:2007lbz
;
Belle:2007oni
;
LHCb:2017swz
;
LHCb:2022lff
;
Belle:2019abe
;
CLEO:1989xsn
;
PDG
.
So far only the
\bar{B}^{0}\to p\bar{p}
and
B^{-}\to\Lambda\bar{p}
modes have been observed
LHCb:2016nbc
;
LHCb:2017swz
.
As most of the bounds have not been updated over a decade, experimental progress in this sector from LHCb and Belle II in near future is anticipated.

Theoretically two body baryonic
B
decays have beed studied in various approaches, including
pole model
Deshpande:1987nc
;
Jarfi:1990ej
;
Cheng:2001tr
;
Cheng:2001ub
,
sum rule
Chernyak:ag
, diquark
model
Ball:1990fw
;
Chang:2001jt
, flavor symmetry
Gronau:1987xq
;
He:re
;
Sheikholeslami:fa
;
Luo:2003pv
;
Chua:2003it
;
Chua:2013zga
;
Chua:2016aqy
, factorization
He:2006vz
;
Hsiao:2014zza
;
Hsiao:2019wyd
;
Jin:2021onb
and some other calculations
Cheng:2014qxa
.
For some recent reviews, see
review
;
Huang:2021qld
.

In this work we will employ the approach of refs.
Chua:2003it
;
Chua:2013zga
;
Chua:2016aqy
,
which made use of the well established topological amplitude formalism
Zeppenfeld:1980ex
;
Chau:tk
;
Chau:1990ay
;
Gronau:1994rj
;
Gronau:1995hn
;
Chiang:2004nm
;
Cheng:2014rfa
;
Savage:ub
and asymptotic relations
Brodsky:1980sx
in the large
m_{b}
limit.
Note that the approach successfully predicted the
B^{-}\to\Lambda\bar{p}
rate
Chua:2013zga
using the data of
\bar{B}^{0}\to p\bar{p}
decay
Aaij:2013fta
.

We shall extend the previous study in several aspects.
First, additional topological amplitudes will be introduced in
\bar{B}_{q=u,d,s}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}
decays with
{\rm\bf B}
denoting low lying octet and decuplet baryons.
Second, some of the topological amplitudes have factorization contributions, which can be calculated using factorization approach.
For some important progress of diagrammatic approach with factorization assisted, one is referred to refs.
Li:2012cfa
;
Qin:2013tje
.
Note that
\bar{B}_{u,d,s}
decaying to low lying octet baryon pairs have been studied in refs.
Hsiao:2014zza
;
Jin:2021onb
using factorization approach,
but our formalism is different and, consequently, we will be able to extend the study to include all low lying octet and decuplet baryon pairs.
Third, we will study
B^{-}_{c}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}
decays and will give predictions on rates and direct CP violations.

There are accumulating speculations of new physics effects in rare
B
decays, see, for example,
Altmannshofer:2021qrr
;
Cornella:2021sby
from some recent discussions.
Any test of the Standard Model (SM) should be welcomed.
In this work we try to identify some robust predictions from SM and null tests of the SM in rare
B
decays in the baryonic sector.

The layout of this paper is as following. We give the formalism in Sec. II, which is followed by numerical results on rates and direct CP violations of
\bar{B}_{u,d,s}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}
and
B^{-}_{c}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}
decays in Sec III.
Sec. IV is devoted to discussions and conclusions. We end this paper by two appendices.

II
Formalism

II.1
Topological amplitudes

Figure 1:
Topological diagrams of
(a)
T
(tree), (b)
P
(penguin), (c)
E
(
W
-exchange),
(d)
A
(annihilation), (e)
PA
(penguin annihilation), (f)
P_{EW}
(electroweak penguin)
and (g)
PE
(penguin-exchange)
amplitudes in
\overline{B}
to baryon pair decays.
These are flavor flow diagrams.

The effective weak Hamiltonian for charmless
\bar{B}_{u,d,s}
decays is given by
Buras

\displaystyle H_{\rm eff}=\frac{G_{f}}{\sqrt{2}}\Big\{\sum_{r=u,c}V_{qb}V^{*}_{uq}[c_{1}O^{r}_{1}+c_{2}O^{r}_{2}]-V_{tb}V^{*}_{tq}\sum_{i=3}^{10}c_{i}O_{i}\Big\}+{\rm H.c.},

(1)

where we have
q=d,s
, and

\displaystyle O^{r}_{1}=(\bar{r}b)_{V-A}(\bar{q}r)_{V-A},\quad O^{r}_{2}=(\bar{r}_{\alpha}b_{\beta})_{V-A}(\bar{q}_{\beta}r_{\alpha})_{V-A},

\displaystyle O_{3(5)}=(\bar{q}b)_{V-A}\sum_{q^{\prime}}(\bar{q}^{\prime}q^{\prime})_{V\mp A},\quad O_{4(6)}=(\bar{q}_{\alpha}b_{\beta})_{V-A}\sum_{q^{\prime}}(\bar{q}_{\beta}^{\prime}q_{\alpha}^{\prime})_{V\mp A},

\displaystyle O_{7(9)}=\frac{3}{2}(\bar{q}b)_{V-A}\sum_{q^{\prime}}e_{q^{\prime}}(\bar{q}^{\prime}q^{\prime})_{V\pm A},\quad O_{8(10)}=\frac{3}{2}(\bar{q}_{\alpha}b_{\beta})_{V-A}\sum_{q^{\prime}}e_{q^{\prime}}(\bar{q}_{\beta}^{\prime}q_{\alpha}^{\prime})_{V\pm A},

(2)

with
O_{3-6}
the QCD penguin operators,
O_{7-10}
the electroweak penguin operators, and
(\bar{q}^{\prime}q)_{V\pm A}\equiv\bar{q}^{\prime}\gamma_{\mu}(1+\pm\gamma_{5})q
. The next-to-leading order Wilson coefficients,

\displaystyle c_{1}=1.081,\,c_{2}=-0.190,\,c_{3}=0.014,\,c_{4}=-0.036,\,c_{5}=0.009,\,c_{6}=-0.042,

\displaystyle c_{7}=-0.011\alpha_{EM},\,c_{8}=0.060\alpha_{EM},\,c_{9}=-1.254\alpha_{EM},\,c_{10}=0.223\alpha_{EM},

(3)

are evaluated in the naive dimensional regularization scheme at scale
\mu=4.2
GeV
Beneke:2001ev
.

We follow the approach of
Chua:2003it
;
Chua:2013zga
;
Chua:2016aqy
to decompose
\bar{B}_{q}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}
,
{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}
,
{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}
and
{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}
decay amplitudes, with
q=u,d,s
,
{\cal B}
and
{\cal D}
denoting low-lying octet and decuplet baryons, into topological amplitudes.
We have tree (
T
), penguin (
P
), electroweak penguin(
P_{EW}
),
W
-exchange (
E
) annihilation, penguin-annihilation (
PA
) and penguin-exchange (
PE
) amplitudes, see Fig.
1
for the corresponding diagrams.
For
\Delta S=0
transition, the
tree (
{\cal O}_{T}=O_{1,2}
), penguin (
{\cal O}_{P}=O_{3-6}
) and electroweak penguin (
{\cal O}_{EWP}=O_{7-10}
) operators in Hamiltonian has the following flavor structure,

\displaystyle{\cal O}_{T}\sim(\bar{u}b)(\bar{d}u)=H^{ik}_{j}(\bar{q}_{i}b)(\bar{q}_{k}q^{j}),\quad{\cal O}_{P}\sim(\bar{d}b)(\bar{q}_{i}q^{i})=H^{k}(\bar{q}_{k}b)(\bar{q}_{i}q^{i}),

\displaystyle{\cal O}_{EWP}\sim Q_{j}(\bar{d}b)(\bar{q}_{j}q^{j})=H_{EW}{}^{ik}_{j}(\bar{q}_{i}b)(\bar{q}_{k}q^{j}),

(4)

with

\displaystyle H^{12}_{1}=1=H^{2},H_{EW}{}^{2k}_{j}=Q_{j}\delta^{k}_{j}\,\,\,{\rm otherwise}\,\,\,H^{ik}_{j}=H_{EW}{}^{ik}_{j}=H^{k}=0.

(5)

Following ref.
Chua:2003it
by suitably matching the
q_{k}q_{i}q_{l}
flavor to decuplet and octet baryon fields,
we obtain the following effective Hamiltonian for
\bar{B}_{q}\to{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}},{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}},{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}
and
{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}
decays,

\displaystyle H^{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}_{\rm eff}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 6\,T_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H^{ik}_{j}\overline{\cal D}_{ikl}{\cal D}^{ljm}+6P_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H^{k}\overline{\cal D}_{kil}{\cal D}^{lim}+6E_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}\,\overline{B}_{k}H^{ik}_{j}\overline{\cal D}_{ilm}{\cal D}^{mlj}

(6)

\displaystyle+6A_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}\,\overline{B}_{i}H^{ik}_{j}\overline{\cal D}_{klm}{\cal D}^{mlj}+2PA_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}\,\overline{B}_{k}H^{k}\overline{\cal D}_{lmn}{\cal D}^{nml}+6\,P_{EW{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H^{ik}_{EW\,j}\overline{\cal D}_{ikl}{\cal D}^{ljm}

\displaystyle+6PE_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H^{k}\overline{\cal D}_{kil}{\cal D}^{lim},

\displaystyle H^{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}_{\rm eff}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{6}\,T_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H^{ik}_{j}\epsilon_{ika}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{l}{\cal D}^{ljm}-2\sqrt{6}\,T_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H^{ik}_{j}\epsilon_{akl}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{i}{\cal D}^{ljm}

(7)

\displaystyle-\sqrt{6}P_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H^{k}\epsilon_{kia}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{l}{\cal D}^{lim}-\sqrt{6}E_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}\,\overline{B}_{k}H^{ik}_{j}\epsilon_{ila}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{m}{\cal D}^{mlj}-\sqrt{6}A_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}\,\overline{B}_{i}H^{ik}_{j}\epsilon_{kla}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{m}{\cal D}^{mlj}

\displaystyle-\sqrt{6}\,P_{1EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H_{EW}{}^{ik}_{j}\epsilon_{ika}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{l}{\cal D}^{ljm}-2\sqrt{6}\,P_{2EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H_{EW}{}^{ik}_{j}\epsilon_{akl}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{i}{\cal D}^{ljm}

\displaystyle-\sqrt{6}PE_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H^{k}\epsilon_{kia}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{l}{\cal D}^{lim},

\displaystyle H^{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}_{\rm eff}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{6}\,T_{1{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H^{ik}_{j}\overline{\cal D}_{ikl}\epsilon^{ljb}{\cal B}^{m}_{b}+\sqrt{6}\,T_{2{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H^{ik}_{j}\overline{\cal D}_{ikl}\epsilon^{bjm}{\cal B}^{l}_{b}

(8)

\displaystyle+\sqrt{6}P_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H^{k}\overline{\cal D}_{kil}\epsilon^{bim}{\cal B}^{l}_{b}+\sqrt{6}E_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}\,\overline{B}_{k}H^{ik}_{j}\overline{\cal D}_{ilm}\epsilon^{bli}{\cal B}^{m}_{b}+\sqrt{6}A_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}\,\overline{B}_{i}H^{ik}_{j}\overline{\cal D}_{klm}\epsilon^{bli}{\cal B}^{m}_{b}

\displaystyle-\sqrt{6}\,P_{1EW{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H_{EW}{}^{ik}_{j}\overline{\cal D}_{ikl}\epsilon^{ljb}{\cal B}^{m}_{b}+\sqrt{6}\,P_{2EW{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H_{EW}{}^{ik}_{j}\overline{\cal D}_{ikl}\epsilon^{bjm}{\cal B}^{l}_{b}

\displaystyle+\sqrt{6}PE_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H^{k}\overline{\cal D}_{kil}\epsilon^{bim}{\cal B}^{l}_{b},

and

\displaystyle H^{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}_{\rm eff}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H^{ik}_{j}\epsilon_{ika}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{l}\epsilon^{ljb}{\cal B}^{m}_{b}-T_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H^{ik}_{j}\epsilon_{ika}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{l}\epsilon^{bjm}{\cal B}^{l}_{b}

(9)

\displaystyle+2T_{3{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H^{ik}_{j}\epsilon_{akl}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{i}\epsilon^{ljb}{\cal B}^{m}_{b}-2T_{4{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H^{ik}_{j}\epsilon_{akl}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{i}\epsilon^{bjm}{\cal B}^{l}_{b}

\displaystyle-5P_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H^{k}\epsilon_{kia}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{l}\epsilon^{lib}{\cal B}^{m}_{b}-P_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H^{k}\epsilon_{kia}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{l}\epsilon^{bim}{\cal B}^{l}_{b}

\displaystyle-5E_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}\,\overline{B}_{k}H^{ik}_{j}\epsilon_{ila}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{m}\epsilon^{mlb}{\cal B}^{j}_{b}-E_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}\,\overline{B}_{k}H^{ik}_{j}\epsilon_{ila}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{m}\epsilon^{blj}{\cal B}^{l}_{m}

\displaystyle-5A_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}\,\overline{B}_{i}H^{ik}_{j}\epsilon_{kla}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{m}\epsilon^{mlb}{\cal B}^{j}_{b}-A_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}\,\overline{B}_{i}H^{ik}_{j}\epsilon_{kla}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{m}\epsilon^{blj}{\cal B}^{l}_{m}

\displaystyle+P_{1EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H_{EW}{}^{ik}_{j}\epsilon_{ika}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{l}\epsilon^{ljb}{\cal B}^{m}_{b}-P_{2EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H_{EW}{}^{ik}_{j}\epsilon_{ika}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{l}\epsilon^{bjm}{\cal B}^{l}_{b}

\displaystyle+2P_{3EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H_{EW}{}^{ik}_{j}\epsilon_{akl}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{i}\epsilon^{ljb}{\cal B}^{m}_{b}-2P_{4EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H_{EW}{}^{ik}_{j}\epsilon_{akl}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{i}\epsilon^{bjm}{\cal B}^{l}_{b}

\displaystyle-PA_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}\,\overline{B}_{k}H^{k}\epsilon_{lma}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{n}\epsilon^{nmb}{\cal B}^{l}_{b}-5PE_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H^{k}\epsilon_{kia}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{l}\epsilon^{lib}{\cal B}^{m}_{b}

\displaystyle-PE_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}\,\overline{B}_{m}H^{k}\epsilon_{kia}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{l}\epsilon^{bim}{\cal B}^{l}_{b}.

with
{\overline{B}}_{m}=\left(B^{-},\overline{B}{}^{0},\overline{B}{}^{0}_{s}\right)
,

{\cal D}^{111}=\Delta^{++}
,

{\cal D}^{112}=\Delta^{+}/\sqrt{3}
,

{\cal D}^{122}=\Delta^{0}/\sqrt{3}
,

{\cal D}^{222}=\Delta^{-}
,

{\cal D}^{113}=\Sigma^{*-}/\sqrt{3}
,

{\cal D}^{123}=\Sigma^{*0}/\sqrt{6}
,

{\cal D}^{223}=\Sigma^{*-}/\sqrt{3}
,

{\cal D}^{133}=\Xi^{*0}/\sqrt{3}
,

{\cal D}^{233}=\Xi^{*-}/\sqrt{3}

{\cal D}^{333}=\Omega^{-}
,
and

\displaystyle{\mathcal{B}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}{{\Sigma^{0}}\over\sqrt{2}}+{{\Lambda}\over\sqrt{6}}&{\Sigma^{+}}&{p}\\
{\Sigma^{-}}&-{{\Sigma^{0}}\over\sqrt{2}}+{{\Lambda}\over\sqrt{6}}&{n}\\
{\Xi^{-}}&{\Xi^{0}}&-\sqrt{2\over 3}{\Lambda}\end{array}\right),

(see, for example
text
). Note that the penguin exchange amplitudes,
PE
are new and the coefficients of
PA
are adjusted (by a factor of
1/3
) for later purpose.

The above formalism can be extended to study
B^{-}_{c}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}
decays. The Hamiltonian governing the decays has the following flavor structure,

\displaystyle{\cal O}_{T}\sim(\bar{c}b)(\bar{d}u)=H^{ck}_{j}(\bar{c}b)(\bar{q}_{k}q^{j}),\quad H^{c2}_{1}=1,\,\,\,{\rm otherwise}\,\,\,H^{ck}_{j}=0.

(14)

Hence the effective Hamiltonian for
\bar{B}_{c}\to{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}},{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}},{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}
and
{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}
decays can be constructed similarly giving

\displaystyle H^{c{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}}_{\rm eff}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 6A^{c}_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}\,\overline{B}_{c}H^{ck}_{j}\overline{\cal D}_{klm}{\cal D}^{mlj},

(15)

\displaystyle H^{c{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}}_{\rm eff}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{6}A^{c}_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}\,\overline{B}_{c}H^{ck}_{j}\overline{\cal D}_{klm}\epsilon^{bli}{\cal B}^{m}_{b},

(16)

\displaystyle H^{c{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}}_{\rm eff}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{6}A^{c}_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}\,\overline{B}_{c}H^{ck}_{j}\epsilon_{kla}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{m}{\cal D}^{mlj},

(17)

and

\displaystyle H^{c{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}_{\rm eff}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-5A^{c}_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}\,\overline{B}_{c}H^{ck}_{j}\epsilon_{kla}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{m}\epsilon^{mlb}{\cal B}^{j}_{b}-A^{c}_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}\,\overline{B}_{c}H^{ck}_{j}\epsilon_{kla}\overline{\cal B}^{a}_{m}\epsilon^{blj}{\cal B}^{l}_{m}.

(18)

The above results for
\bar{B}_{u,d,s}
and
B^{-}_{c}
decays are for
\Delta S=0
transitions.
In the case of
\Delta S=-1
transition,
we put a prime in topological amplitudes and use

H^{13}_{1}=1=H^{3}
,
H_{EW}{}^{3k}_{j}=Q_{j}\delta^{k}_{j}
and
H^{c3}_{1}=1
for non-vanishing elements, instead.

The
\bar{B}_{q},\bar{B}_{c}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime},{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}^{\prime},{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}
and
{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}^{\prime}
decay amplitudes obtained using these effective Hamiltonian are collected in App.
A
. Since the flavor flow structures of penguin exchange diagrams and penguin diagrams are identical, see Fig.
1
(b) and (g), these two topological amplitudes always occur in the combination of
P^{(\prime)}_{i}+PE^{(\prime)}_{i}
in the decay amplitudes.
It should be noted that although the above constructions make use of SU(3) symmetry, they are use as tools, as bookkeeping devices, to obtain flavor flow structure of the decay amplitudes.
Once the flavor flow structure is obtained, SU(3) breaking effects, through masses, decay constants and so on, in these topological amplitudes can be imposed.
Note that annihilation diagrams only exist in
B^{-}
and
B^{-}_{c}
decays, while exchange diagrams only exist in
B^{0}_{d}
and
B^{0}_{s}
decays and penguin-annihilation diagrams only exist in
\bar{B}^{0}_{d(s)}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}
and
{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}
decays, where the final state anti-baryon is the anti-particle of the associated final state baryon.

II.2
Factorization contributions to
A^{(\prime)}
,
E^{(\prime)}
,
PE^{(\prime)}
and
PA^{(\prime)}

A typical factorizable
\bar{B}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}
decay amplitude has the following expression, which is similar to the mesonic case
Beneke:2001ev
:

\displaystyle A_{\rm fac}\left(\bar{B}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg\{V_{ub}V_{uq}^{*}[a_{1}(\bar{q}u)_{V-A}\otimes(\bar{u}b)_{V-A}+a_{2}(\bar{u}u)_{V-A}\otimes(\bar{q}b)_{V-A}]

(19)

\displaystyle-V_{tb}V_{tq}^{*}\Big[a_{3}\sum_{q^{\prime}}(\bar{q}^{\prime}q^{\prime})_{V-A}\otimes(\bar{q}b)_{V-A}+a_{4}\sum_{q^{\prime}}(\bar{q}q^{\prime})_{V-A}\otimes(\bar{q}^{\prime}b)_{V-A}

\displaystyle\quad+a_{5}\sum_{q^{\prime}}(\bar{q}^{\prime}q^{\prime})_{V+A}\otimes(\bar{q}b)_{V-A}-2a_{6}\sum_{q^{\prime}}(\bar{q}q^{\prime})_{S+P}\otimes(\bar{q}^{\prime}b)_{S-P}\Big]\bigg\}\,,

where
{\cal O}_{1}\otimes{\cal O}_{2}
is the shorthand of

\displaystyle{\cal O}_{1}\otimes{\cal O}_{2}\equiv\langle{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}|{\cal O}_{1}|0\rangle\langle 0|{\cal O}_{2}|\bar{B}\rangle

(20)

and we neglect the contributions from electroweak penguin operators in the factorization calculation.
Note that in factorization calculation the electroweak penguin operators contribute to electroweak-exchange and electroweak-annihilation diagrams, which are negligible comparing to the topological amplitudes generated from tree and strong penguin operators, and these topological amplitudes are not considered in this work.
In the leading order, the coefficients

a_{i}
are defined in terms of the effective Wilson coefficients
c_{i}
as

\displaystyle a^{\rm LO}_{i={\rm odd}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle c_{i}+c_{i+1}/N_{c},

\displaystyle a^{\rm LO}_{i={\rm even}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle c_{i}+c_{i-1}/N_{c}.

(21)

Contributions beyond the leading order will neglected in this work.
It will be useful to express the above factorization amplitudes according to the decaying mesons, giving

\displaystyle A_{\rm fac}\left(B^{-}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg\{V_{ub}V_{uq}^{*}a_{1}(\bar{q}u)_{V-A}\otimes(\bar{u}b)_{V-A}-V_{tb}V_{tq}^{*}\Big[a_{4}(\bar{q}u)_{V-A}\otimes(\bar{u}b)_{V-A}

(22)

\displaystyle\quad-2a_{6}(\bar{q}u)_{S+P}\otimes(\bar{u}b)_{S-P}\Big]\bigg\}\,,

\displaystyle A_{\rm fac}\left(\bar{B}_{d}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg\{V_{ub}V_{uq}^{*}a_{2}(\bar{u}u)_{V-A}\otimes(\bar{q}b)_{V-A}\delta_{qd}

(23)

\displaystyle-V_{tb}V_{tq}^{*}\Big[a_{3}\sum_{q^{\prime}}(\bar{q}^{\prime}q^{\prime})_{V-A}\otimes(\bar{q}b)_{V-A}\delta_{qd}+a_{4}(\bar{q}d)_{V-A}\otimes(\bar{d}b)_{V-A}

\displaystyle\quad+a_{5}\sum_{q^{\prime}}(\bar{q}^{\prime}q^{\prime})_{V+A}\otimes(\bar{q}b)_{V-A}\delta_{qd}-2a_{6}(\bar{q}d)_{S+P}\otimes(\bar{d}b)_{S-P}\Big]\bigg\}\,,

\displaystyle A_{\rm fac}\left(\bar{B}_{s}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}\bigg\{V_{ub}V_{uq}^{*}a_{2}(\bar{u}u)_{V-A}\otimes(\bar{q}b)_{V-A}\delta_{qs}

(24)

\displaystyle-V_{tb}V_{tq}^{*}\Big[a_{3}\sum_{q^{\prime}}(\bar{q}^{\prime}q^{\prime})_{V-A}\otimes(\bar{q}b)_{V-A}\delta_{qs}+a_{4}(\bar{q}s)_{V-A}\otimes(\bar{s}b)_{V-A}

\displaystyle\quad+a_{5}\sum_{q^{\prime}}(\bar{q}^{\prime}q^{\prime})_{V+A}\otimes(\bar{q}b)_{V-A}\delta_{qs}-2a_{6}(\bar{q}s)_{S+P}\otimes(\bar{s}b)_{S-P}\Big]\bigg\}\,,

and

\displaystyle A_{\rm fac}\left(B^{-}_{c}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}V_{cb}V_{uq}^{*}a_{1}(\bar{q}u)_{V-A}\otimes(\bar{c}b)_{V-A}.

(25)

By comparing these amplitudes with the topological amplitudes given in App.
A
, we have the following correspondence between topological amplitudes and the factorization amplitudes:

\displaystyle c^{A}_{i,B^{-}_{u}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}}A_{i,B^{-}_{u}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}V_{ub}V_{ud}^{*}\,a_{1}(\bar{d}u)_{V-A}\otimes(\bar{u}b)_{V-A},

\displaystyle c^{A^{c}}_{i,B^{-}_{c}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}}A^{c}_{i,B^{-}_{c}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}V_{cb}V_{ud}^{*}a_{1}(\bar{d}u)_{V-A}\otimes(\bar{c}b)_{V-A},

\displaystyle c^{E}_{i,\bar{B}_{d}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}}E_{i,\bar{B}_{d}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}V_{ub}V_{ud}^{*}a_{2}(\bar{u}u)_{V-A}\otimes(\bar{d}b)_{V-A},

\displaystyle c^{PE}_{i,\bar{B}_{q^{\prime\prime}}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}}PE_{i,\bar{B}_{q^{\prime\prime}}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}V_{tb}V_{td}^{*}\,[a_{4}(\bar{d}q^{\prime\prime})_{V-A}\otimes(\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}b)_{V-A}-2a_{6}(\bar{d}q^{\prime\prime})_{S+P}\otimes(\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}b)_{S-P}],

\displaystyle c^{PA}_{B^{-}_{d}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}}PA_{B^{-}_{d}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}V_{tb}V_{td}^{*}\,[a_{3}\sum_{q^{\prime}}(\bar{q}^{\prime}q^{\prime})_{V-A}\otimes(\bar{d}b)_{V-A}

(26)

\displaystyle\quad+a_{5}\sum_{q^{\prime}}(\bar{q}^{\prime}q^{\prime})_{V+A}\otimes(\bar{d}b)_{V-A}],

for
\Delta S=0
transition, and

\displaystyle c^{A^{\prime}}_{i,B^{-}_{u}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}}A^{\prime}_{i,B^{-}_{u}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}V_{ub}V_{us}^{*}\,a_{1}(\bar{s}u)_{V-A}\otimes(\bar{u}b)_{V-A},

\displaystyle c^{A^{\prime c}}_{i,B^{-}_{c}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}}A^{\prime c}_{i,B^{-}_{c}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}V_{cb}V_{us}^{*}a_{1}(\bar{s}u)_{V-A}\otimes(\bar{c}b)_{V-A},

\displaystyle c^{E^{\prime}}_{i,\bar{B}_{s}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}}E^{\prime}_{i,\bar{B}_{s}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}V_{ub}V_{us}^{*}a_{2}(\bar{u}u)_{V-A}\otimes(\bar{s}b)_{V-A},

\displaystyle c^{PE^{\prime}}_{i,\bar{B}_{q^{\prime\prime}}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}}PE^{\prime}_{i,\bar{B}_{q^{\prime\prime}}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}V_{tb}V_{ts}^{*}\,[a_{4}(\bar{s}q^{\prime\prime})_{V-A}\otimes(\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}b)_{V-A}-2a_{6}(\bar{s}q^{\prime\prime})_{S+P}\otimes(\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}b)_{S-P}],

\displaystyle c^{PA^{\prime}}_{B^{-}_{s}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}}PA^{\prime}_{B^{-}_{s}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}V_{tb}V_{ts}^{*}\,[a_{3}\sum_{q^{\prime}}(\bar{q}^{\prime}q^{\prime})_{V-A}\otimes(\bar{s}b)_{V-A}

(27)

\displaystyle\quad+a_{5}\sum_{q^{\prime}}(\bar{q}^{\prime}q^{\prime})_{V+A}\otimes(\bar{s}b)_{V-A}],

for
\Delta S=-1
transition,
where the constants
c
are the Clebsch-Gordan coefficients accompanying with the topological amplitudes
in the corresponding
\bar{B}_{q}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}
decay amplitudes as shown in App.
A
and summations over
i
, if necessary, are understood.

Using

\displaystyle\langle 0|(\bar{q}b)_{V-A}|\bar{B}_{q}\rangle=-if_{B_{q}}p_{\mu}

(28)

and equations of motions,
the matrix elements in the above equations can be evaluated as

\displaystyle\langle{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}|(\bar{q}q^{\prime})_{V\mp A}|0\rangle\langle 0|(\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}b)_{V-A}|\bar{B}_{q^{\prime\prime}}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-if_{B_{q^{\prime\prime}}}[(m_{q}-m_{\bar{q}^{\prime}})\langle{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}|(\bar{q}q^{\prime})_{S}|0\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 18.0pt\mp(m_{q}+m_{\bar{q}^{\prime}})\langle{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}|(\bar{q}q^{\prime})_{P}|0\rangle],

\displaystyle\langle{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}|(\bar{q}q^{\prime})_{S+P}|0\rangle\langle 0|(\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}b)_{S-P}|\bar{B}_{q^{\prime\prime}}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle if_{B_{q^{\prime\prime}}}\frac{m^{2}_{B_{q^{\prime\prime}}}}{m_{b}+m_{q^{\prime\prime}}}\langle{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}|(\bar{q}q^{\prime})_{S+P}|0\rangle.

(29)

Hence the above factorization amplitudes can all be expressed
in terms of the
\langle{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}|(\bar{q}q^{\prime})_{S,P}|0\rangle
matrix elements.
For later purpose, we define

\displaystyle r_{{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}}\equiv\frac{\langle{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}|\sum_{q^{\prime}}m_{q^{\prime}}(\bar{q}^{\prime}q^{\prime})_{S-P}|0\rangle}{(\sum_{q^{\prime}}m_{q^{\prime}})\langle{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}|\sum_{q^{\prime}}(\bar{q}^{\prime}q^{\prime})_{S-P}|0\rangle}.

(30)

In the large
m_{B}
limit, there are asymptotic relations between the matrix elements of
\langle{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}|(\bar{q}q^{\prime})_{S,P}|0\rangle
, see App.
B
.
Consequently, the
r_{{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}}
defined in Eq. (
30
) reduces to

\displaystyle r_{{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}}=\frac{m_{u}e_{1}+m_{d}e_{2}+m_{s}e_{3}}{m_{u}+m_{d}+m_{s}},

(31)

with
e_{i}
some constants.
The
(e_{1},e_{2},e_{3})
and
r_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}
for
\bar{B}\to{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}
and
{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}
decays
are given in Table
2
.
Furthermore, in the large
m_{B}
limit, the matrix elemets
\langle{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}|(\bar{q}q^{\prime})_{S,P}|0\rangle
are related,
giving the following asymptotic relations,

\displaystyle A^{(\prime)}_{1,2\,{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A^{(\prime)}_{{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}^{\prime}}=A^{(\prime)}_{{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}}=A^{(\prime)}_{{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}^{\prime}}=A^{(\prime)}_{B^{-}_{u}},

\displaystyle A^{(\prime)c}_{1,2\,{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A^{(\prime)c}_{{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}^{\prime}}=A^{(\prime)c}_{{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}}=A^{(\prime)c}_{{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}^{\prime}}=A^{(\prime)c}_{B^{-}_{c}},

\displaystyle E^{(\prime)}_{1,2\,{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E^{(\prime)}_{{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}^{\prime}}=E^{(\prime)}_{{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}}=E^{(\prime)}_{{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}^{\prime}}=E^{(\prime)}_{\bar{B}^{0}(\bar{B}^{0}_{s})},

\displaystyle PE^{(\prime)}_{1,2\,{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle PE^{(\prime)}_{{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}^{\prime}}=PE^{(\prime)}_{{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}}=PE^{(\prime)}_{{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}^{\prime}}=PE^{(\prime)}_{\bar{B}_{q^{\prime\prime}}},

\displaystyle PA^{(\prime)}_{{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle PA^{(\prime)}_{{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}}=PA^{(\prime)}_{\bar{B}^{0}_{d(s)}},

(32)

with

\displaystyle A_{B^{-}_{u}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-if_{B_{u}}\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}V_{ub}V_{ud}^{*}\,a_{1}f(m^{2}_{B_{u}})\bar{u}(p_{\rm\bf B})[(m_{d}-m_{u})-(m_{d}+m_{u})\gamma_{5}]v(p_{\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}})\,,

\displaystyle A^{c}_{B^{-}_{c}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-if_{B_{c}}\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}V_{cb}V_{ud}^{*}a_{1}f(m^{2}_{B_{c}})\bar{u}(p_{\rm\bf B})[(m_{d}-m_{u})-(m_{d}+m_{u})\gamma_{5}]v(p_{\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}}),

\displaystyle E_{\bar{B}_{d}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle if_{B_{d}}\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}V_{ub}V_{ud}^{*}a_{2}2m_{u}f(m^{2}_{B_{d}})\bar{u}(p_{\rm\bf B})\gamma_{5}v(p_{\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}}),

\displaystyle PE_{\bar{B}_{q^{\prime\prime}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle if_{B_{q^{\prime\prime}}}\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}V_{tb}V_{td}^{*}\,f(m^{2}_{B_{q^{\prime\prime}}})\bar{u}(p_{\rm\bf B})\bigg[\bigg(a_{4}(m_{d}-m_{q^{\prime\prime}})+2a_{6}\frac{m^{2}_{B_{q^{\prime\prime}}}}{m_{b}+m_{q^{\prime\prime}}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\bigg(-a_{4}(m_{d}+m_{q^{\prime\prime}})+2a_{6}\frac{m^{2}_{B_{q^{\prime\prime}}}}{m_{b}+m_{q^{\prime\prime}}}\bigg)\gamma_{5}\bigg]v(p_{\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}}),

\displaystyle PA_{\bar{B}_{d}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-if_{B_{d}}\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}V_{tb}V_{td}^{*}\,f(m^{2}_{B_{d}})(a_{3}-a_{5})2(m_{u}+m_{d}+m_{s})r_{{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}}\,\bar{u}(p_{\rm\bf B})\gamma_{5}v(p_{\overline{\rm\bf B}}),

(33)

and

\displaystyle A^{\prime}_{B^{-}_{u}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-if_{B_{u}}\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}V_{ub}V_{us}^{*}\,a_{1}f(m^{2}_{B_{u}})\bar{u}(p_{\rm\bf B})[(m_{s}-m_{u})-(m_{s}+m_{u})\gamma_{5}]v(p_{\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}})\,,

\displaystyle A^{\prime c}_{B^{-}_{c}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-if_{B_{c}}\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}V_{cb}V_{us}^{*}\,a_{1}f(m^{2}_{B_{c}})\bar{u}(p_{\rm\bf B})[(m_{s}-m_{u})-(m_{s}+m_{u})\gamma_{5}]v(p_{\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}})\,,

\displaystyle E^{\prime}_{\bar{B}_{s}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle if_{B_{s}}\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}V_{ub}V_{us}^{*}a_{2}2m_{u}f(m^{2}_{B_{s}})\bar{u}(p_{\rm\bf B})\gamma_{5}v(p_{\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}}),

\displaystyle PE^{\prime}_{\bar{B}_{q^{\prime\prime}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle if_{B_{q^{\prime\prime}}}\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}V_{tb}V_{ts}^{*}f(m^{2}_{B_{q^{\prime\prime}}})\,\bar{u_{\rm\bf B}}(p_{\rm\bf B})\bigg[\bigg(a_{4}(m_{s}-m_{q^{\prime\prime}})+2a_{6}\frac{m^{2}_{B_{q^{\prime\prime}}}}{m_{b}+m_{q^{\prime\prime}}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\bigg(-a_{4}(m_{s}+m_{q^{\prime\prime}})+2a_{6}\frac{m^{2}_{B_{q^{\prime\prime}}}}{m_{b}+m_{q^{\prime\prime}}}\bigg)\gamma_{5}\bigg]v(p_{\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}}),

\displaystyle PA^{\prime}_{\bar{B}_{s}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-if_{B_{s}}\frac{G_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}V_{tb}V_{ts}^{*}\,(a_{3}-a_{5})f(m^{2}_{B_{s}})\,2(m_{u}+m_{d}+m_{s})r_{{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}}\bar{u}(p_{\rm\bf B})\gamma_{5}v(p_{\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}}).

(34)

Note that the Clebsch-Gordan coefficients in Eqs. (
26
) and (
27
) canceled out in the above equations.
Furthermore,
A^{(\prime)}
,
E^{(\prime)}
and
PA^{(\prime)}
are proportional to light quark masses and are vanishing in the chiral limit, while
PE^{(\prime)}
are not vanishing.
The chiral limits of these topological amplitudes are consistent with the findings in
Chua:2013zga
;
Chua:2016aqy
, except
PE^{(\prime)}
, which were not considered in
Chua:2013zga
;
Chua:2016aqy
.

Through SU(3) symmetry the matrix element
\langle\Lambda\overline{p}|\bar{s}\gamma_{\mu}u|0\rangle
can be related to proton electromagnetic (EM) form factors
Chua:2002yd
.
The time-like proton EM form factors were fitted in ref.
Chua:2001vh
using data from ref.
EM
.
By employing the fitted proton electromagnetic form factors from ref.
Chua:2001vh
and by matching with the following matrix element,

\displaystyle\langle\Lambda\overline{p}|\bar{s}u|0\rangle=3\sqrt{\frac{3}{2}}f(q^{2})\bar{u}(p_{\Lambda})v(p_{\overline{p}}),

(35)

in the asymptotic limit, we obtain

\displaystyle f(q^{2})=-\frac{1}{3}\frac{m_{\Lambda}-m_{p}}{m_{s}-m_{u}}\,G^{p}_{M}(q^{2}),

(36)

where
G^{p}_{M}
is the time-like magnetic form factor of proton.
The central values of the form factors
f(q^{2})
at various
B
meson masses, by using the
G^{p}_{M}(q^{2})
in ref.
Chua:2001vh
and the quark masses at
\mu=4.2
GeV (see the next section for the quark masses used) are shown in Table
3
.
Note that the values of the form factors (except the one for
B^{-}_{c}
) in the table are of the same order to those obtained in a recent work using MIT-bag model calculation
Jin:2021onb
.

II.3
Specifying topological amplitudes

In the large
m_{B}
limit, the chirality nature of weak and strong interactions provides asymptotic relations
Brodsky:1980sx
giving
Chua:2003it
;
Chua:2013zga
;
Chua:2016aqy
:

\displaystyle T^{(\prime)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T^{(\prime)}_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}},2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}},3{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}},4{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}=T^{(\prime)}_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}},2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}}=T^{(\prime)}_{1{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}},2{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}}=T^{(\prime)}_{{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}},

\displaystyle P^{(\prime)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P^{(\prime)}_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}},2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}=P^{(\prime)}_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}=P^{(\prime)}_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}=P^{(\prime)}_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}},

\displaystyle P^{(\prime)}_{EW}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P^{(\prime)}_{1EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}},2EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}},3EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}},4EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}=P^{(\prime)}_{1EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}},2EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}}=P^{(\prime)}_{1EW{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}},2EW{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}}=P^{(\prime)}_{EW{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}},

(37)

and those shown in Eq. (
32
).
Note that the relations on
PE^{(\prime)}
,
E^{(\prime)}

A^{(\prime)}
and
PA^{(\prime)}
in
Eq. (
32
) are new.

The tree, penguin and electroweak penguin amplitudes are estimated to be
Chua:2016aqy

\displaystyle T^{(\prime)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle V_{ub}V^{*}_{ud(s)}\frac{G_{f}}{\sqrt{2}}(c_{1}+c_{2})\chi\bar{u}^{\prime}(1-\gamma_{5})v,

\displaystyle P^{(\prime)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-V_{tb}V^{*}_{td(s)}\frac{G_{f}}{\sqrt{2}}[c_{3}+c_{4}+\kappa_{1}c_{5}+\kappa_{2}c_{6}]\chi\bar{u}^{\prime}(1-\gamma_{5})v,

\displaystyle P^{(\prime)}_{EW}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{2}V_{tb}V^{*}_{td(s)}\frac{G_{f}}{\sqrt{2}}[c_{9}+c_{10}+\kappa_{1}c_{7}+\kappa_{2}c_{8}]\chi\bar{u}^{\prime}(1-\gamma_{5})v,

(38)

where
c_{i}
are the next-to-leading order Wilson coefficients.
The parameters
\kappa_{i}
are expected to be of
{\cal O}(1)
and, for simplicity, we assume
\kappa_{1}=\kappa_{2}=\kappa
.
We will extract
\chi
and
\kappa
from the latest data on
\overline{B}{}^{0}\to p\bar{p}
and
B^{-}\to\Lambda\bar{p}
decay rates.
From the above equation we see that
\chi
and
\kappa
are correlated.
In our numerical study we assume
\chi
and
\kappa
to be real and positive for simplicity.
This assumption will be relaxed in the uncertainty estimation by introducing a relative phase between
T^{(\prime)}
and
P^{(\prime)}+PE^{(\prime)}
(recall that
P
and
PE
always come together in this combination) and we will see that the phase does not sizably affect the
\bar{B}^{0}\to p\bar{p}
and
B^{-}\to\Lambda\bar{p}
rates.

Following ref.
Chua:2016aqy
we apply the following corrections to
T^{(\prime)}_{i}
,
P^{(\prime)}_{i}
and
P^{(\prime)}_{EWi}
to the asymptotic relations, Eq. (
37
), to account for the finite
m_{B}
effects, which are estimated to be
{\cal O}(m_{\bf B}/m_{B})
with
m_{\bf B}
the baryon mass, giving

\displaystyle T^{(\prime)}_{i}=(1+r_{t,i}^{(\prime)})T^{(\prime)},\,P^{(\prime)}_{i}=(1+r_{p,i}^{(\prime)})P^{(\prime)}_{,}\,P^{(\prime)}_{EWi}=(1+r_{ewp,i}^{(\prime)})P^{(\prime)}_{EW},

(39)

and

\displaystyle|r_{t,i}^{(\prime)}|,|r_{p,i}^{(\prime)}|,|r_{ewp,i}^{(\prime)}|\leq m_{p}/m_{B}.

(40)

The above parameters
r^{(\prime)}
can have phases
and the
r^{(\prime)}
for
\bar{u}^{\prime}u
and
\bar{u}^{\prime}\gamma_{5}u
terms are independent.
Likewise,
for penguin exchange amplitudes, we use

\displaystyle PE^{(\prime)}_{i}\equiv(1+r_{pe,i}^{(\prime)})PE^{(\prime)}_{\bar{B}_{q^{\prime\prime}}}

(41)

to estimate the corrections to the asymptotic relations in Eq. (
32
).
Note that the amplitudes are proportional to the form factor
f(m^{2}_{B})
in Table
3
and the above corrections should include the uncertainty in the form factors.
We assign
|r^{(\prime)}_{pe,i}|\leq 0.5
.

Figure 2:
The chiral structure of an exchange diagram without chiral flip (for simplicity, we plot a spacelike diagram). It needs a chiral flip to contribute to
\bar{B}
decays.

For annihilation, exchange and penguin annihilation-amplitudes, the situation is more complicate.
For example, as shown in Fig.
2
, an exchange diagram without chiral flip cannot produce
\bar{u}v
or
\bar{u}\gamma_{5}v
structure and hence cannot contribute to
\bar{B}_{d,s}
decays.
To overcome that we need to introduce chiral flip. There are two ways to generate a chiral flip, either by quark mass or by baryon mass.
As one can see from Eqs. (
33
) and (
34
), the factorization amplitudes in annihilation,
exchange and penguin-annihilation amplitudes use quark masses to generate the chiral flips,
while it is possible to have non-factorization contributions generating the chiral flip through
\Lambda_{\rm QCD}/m_{b}
.
Indeed, it is well known that the majority of the mass of a baryon does not come from quark mass, but from strong interaction.
Therefore,
\Lambda_{\rm QCD}
can play the role of baryon mass in chiral flip.
To estimate the corrections to the asymptotic relations in Eq. (
32
) for annihilation, exchange and penguin-annihilation amplitudes,
we use the following equations,

\displaystyle A^{(\prime)}_{i}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle(1+r_{a,i}^{(\prime)})A^{(\prime)}_{B^{-}_{u}}+\eta^{(\prime)}_{a,i}\frac{\Lambda_{\rm QCD}}{m_{b}}T^{(\prime)},

\displaystyle A^{c(\prime)}_{i}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle(1+r_{a,i}^{c(\prime)})A^{c(\prime)}_{B^{-}_{c}}+\eta^{c(\prime)}_{a,i}\frac{\Lambda_{\rm QCD}}{m_{b}}T^{(\prime)}_{c},

\displaystyle E^{(\prime)}_{i}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle(1+r_{e,i}^{(\prime)})E^{(\prime)}_{\bar{B}^{0}(\bar{B}^{0}_{s})}+\eta^{(\prime)}_{e,i}\frac{\Lambda_{\rm QCD}}{m_{b}}T^{(\prime)},

\displaystyle PA^{(\prime)}_{{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}},{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle(1+r_{pa,i}^{(\prime)})PA^{(\prime)}_{\bar{B}^{0}_{d(s)}}+\eta^{(\prime)}_{pa}\frac{\Lambda_{\rm QCD}}{m_{b}}P^{(\prime)},

(42)

where terms with
\Lambda_{\rm QCD}/m_{b}
are estimations of non-factorization contributions.
Since
T^{(\prime)}
and
P^{(\prime)}
are non-factorizable, it is natural to use them in the above estimation.
Note that
T^{(\prime)}_{c}
is
T^{(\prime)}
but with the Cabibbo–Kobayashi–Maskawa (CKM) factor
V_{ub}
replaced by
V_{cb}
.
We assign
|r^{(\prime)}_{a,i}|
,
|r^{(\prime)}_{e,i}|
,
|r^{(\prime)}_{pa,i}|
,
|r^{c(\prime)}_{a,i}|\leq 0.5
and
|\eta^{(\prime)}_{a,i}|
,
|\eta^{c(\prime)}_{a,i}|
,
|\eta^{(\prime)}_{e,i}|
and
|\eta^{(\prime)}_{pa}|\leq 1
.
Numerically we take
\Lambda_{\rm QCD}=292
MeV
ParticleDataGroup:2018ovx
.
Note that we will separate penguin, penguin-exchange and penguin-annihilation amplitudes into
u
-penguin and
c
-penguin contributions and their
r
s and
\eta
s will be varying separately.
Furthermore, all
r
s and
\eta
s for
\bar{u}^{\prime}u
and
\bar{u}^{\prime}\gamma_{5}u
terms are independent.

We are now ready to perform numerical study using the above equations and the amplitudes given in App.
A
.

III
Numerical Results on Rates and Direct
CP
Asymmetries

Numerical results on rates and direct
CP
asymmetries will be presented in this section.
In our numerical study, masses of mesons and baryons are taken from ref.
PDG
.
In addition, quark masses and decay constants are taken from the central values given in ref.
PDG
, explicitly, we use

m_{u}=1.86
MeV,
m_{d}=4.02
MeV,
m_{s}=79.98
MeV,
m_{b}=4.2
GeV, at
\mu=4.2
GeV,

f_{B_{u}}=190
MeV,
f_{B_{d}}=190
MeV and
f_{B_{s}}=230
MeV.
For the
B_{c}
decay constant, we follow ref.
Narison:2014ska
and use
f_{B_{c}}=436
MeV.
CKM matrix elements are from the latest fit in ref.
CKMfitter
.

III.1
Sizes of topological amplitudes

Using the recent data on the
\overline{B}{}^{0}\to p\bar{p}
rate and the
B^{-}\to\Lambda\bar{p}
rate,
the unknown parameters
\chi
and
\kappa
in asymptotic amplitudes , Eq. (
38
), are fitted to be
1
1

1

As noted previously
\chi
and
\kappa
are correlated. We find that with
\chi=(4.495+0.354R\cos\lambda-0.003R\sin\lambda)\times 10^{-3}
GeV and
\kappa=1.441-0.000R\cos\lambda+0.573R\sin\lambda
, the experimental rates
{\cal B}(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to p\bar{p})=(1.27\pm 0.14)\times 10^{-8}
and
{\cal B}(B^{-}\to\Lambda\bar{p})=(2.4^{+1.0}_{-0.9})\times 10^{-7}
can be reproduced with
0\leq R\leq 1
and
0\leq\lambda\leq 2\pi
. Note that
R=0
does not correspond to the experimental central values of the decay rates.

\displaystyle\chi=(4.50^{+0.25}_{-0.26})\times 10^{-3}~{\rm GeV}^{2},\qquad\kappa=1.47^{+0.55}_{-0.60}.

(43)

These values are similar to those given in ref.
Chua:2016aqy
,
where the values were
\chi=(5.08^{+1.12}_{-1.02})\times 10^{-3}~{\rm GeV}^{2}

and

\kappa=1.92^{+0.39}_{-0.46}
.
The value of
\chi
is reduced as the experimental
\overline{B}{}^{0}\to p\bar{p}
rate is reduced. While
\kappa
is reduced as
PE^{\prime}
also contributes to the
B^{-}\to\Lambda\bar{p}
rate and hence reduces the contribution from
P^{\prime}
. Note that the
\kappa
in the above equation is closer to 1 and hence agrees better with our expectation.

As noted previously
P^{(\prime)}
and
PE^{(\prime)}
always come in the combination of
P^{(\prime)}+PE^{(\prime)}
.
Therefore it is
T^{(\prime)}
and
P^{(\prime)}+PE^{(\prime)}
that are determined from the data.
It will be useful to see their ratios.
The penguin-tree (tree-penguin) and penguin-exchange-penguin ratios
for
\Delta S=0
(
-1
) transitions are found to be

\displaystyle\left|\frac{P_{\bar{B}_{d}}+PE_{\bar{B}_{d}}}{T_{\bar{B}_{d}}}\right|=0.26\pm 0.05,\quad\left|\frac{T^{\prime}_{B^{-}}}{P^{\prime}_{B^{-}}+PE^{\prime}_{B^{-}}}\right|=0.19^{+0.05}_{-0.03},

(44)

and

\displaystyle\left|\frac{PE^{(\prime)}_{B^{-}}}{P^{(\prime)}_{B^{-}}+PE^{(\prime)}_{B^{-}}}\right|=0.27^{+0.07}_{-0.04},\quad\left|\frac{PE^{(\prime)}_{\bar{B}_{d}}}{P^{(\prime)}_{\bar{B}_{d}}+PE^{(\prime)}_{\bar{B}_{d}}}\right|=0.27^{+0.07}_{-0.04},\quad\left|\frac{PE^{(\prime)}_{\bar{B}_{s}}}{P^{(\prime)}_{\bar{B}_{s}}+PE^{(\prime)}_{\bar{B}_{s}}}\right|=0.30^{+0.07}_{-0.04},

(45)

where the errors reflect the uncertainties in
\chi
and
\kappa
and we keep only the dominant contribution in
PE^{(\prime)}
.
Note that the
|PE^{(\prime)}|/|P^{(\prime)}+PE^{(\prime)}|
ratio is about 30%.
It is interesting that the
PE^{(\prime)}
from factorization contribution is non-negligible comparing to

P^{(\prime)}
. This agrees with some early studies, although they did not identify the contribution as
PE^{(\prime)}

Hsiao:2014zza
;
Jin:2021onb
.

We now discuss the sizes of annihilation, exchange and penguin-annihilation amplitudes with respect to the sizes of penguin-exchange amplitudes in factorization amplitudes.
Using Eqs. (
33
) and (
34
), we see that the ratio of annihilation and penguin-exchange factorization amplitudes for
\Delta S=0
transition is given by

\displaystyle\frac{A_{B^{-}}}{PE_{B^{-}}}\simeq-\frac{V_{ub}V^{*}_{ud}}{V_{tb}V^{*}_{td}}\frac{a_{1}}{a_{6}}\frac{m_{b}+m_{u}}{2m^{2}_{B^{-}}}\frac{\bar{u}(p_{\rm\bf B})[(m_{d}-m_{u})-(m_{d}+m_{u})\gamma_{5}]v(p_{\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}})}{\bar{u}(p_{\rm\bf B})(1+\gamma_{5})v(p_{\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}})},

(46)

where we keep only the dominant term in
PE
.
Note that the form factor
f(q^{2})
are cancelled out in the ratio. Furthermore, in the decay rate, the
\bar{u}v
and
\bar{u}\gamma_{5}v
terms in the decay amplitude do not interfere. Hence it is legitimate to consider their ratios separately, namely

\displaystyle\frac{(A_{B^{-}})_{S}}{(PE_{B^{-}})_{S}}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle-\frac{V_{ub}V^{*}_{ud}}{V_{tb}V^{*}_{td}}\frac{a_{1}}{a_{6}}\frac{m_{b}+m_{u}}{2m^{2}_{B^{-}}}\frac{\bar{u}(p_{\rm\bf B})[(m_{d}-m_{u})]v(p_{\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}})}{\bar{u}(p_{\rm\bf B})v(p_{\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}})}

(47)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{V_{ub}V^{*}_{ud}}{V_{tb}V^{*}_{td}}\frac{a_{1}}{a_{6}}\frac{(m_{b}+m_{u})(m_{d}-m_{u})}{2m^{2}_{B^{-}}},

and

\displaystyle\frac{(A_{B^{-}})_{P}}{(PE_{B^{-}})_{P}}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle-\frac{V_{ub}V^{*}_{ud}}{V_{tb}V^{*}_{td}}\frac{a_{1}}{a_{6}}\frac{m_{b}+m_{u}}{2m^{2}_{B^{-}}}\frac{\bar{u}(p_{\rm\bf B})[-(m_{d}+m_{u})\gamma_{5}]v(p_{\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}})}{\bar{u}(p_{\rm\bf B})\gamma_{5}v(p_{\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}})}

(48)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{V_{ub}V^{*}_{ud}}{V_{tb}V^{*}_{td}}\frac{a_{1}}{a_{6}}\frac{(m_{b}+m_{u})(m_{d}+m_{u})}{2m^{2}_{B^{-}}}.

It should be noted that the ratio of
|(PE_{\bar{B}_{q}})_{S}|
and
|(PE_{\bar{B}_{q}})_{P}|
are of order 1, as

\displaystyle\frac{|(PE_{\bar{B}_{q}})_{S}|}{|(PE_{\bar{B}_{q}})_{P}|}=\sqrt{\frac{m^{2}_{B_{q}}-(m_{\bf B}+m_{\bf{\overline{B}}^{\prime}})^{2}}{m^{2}_{B_{q}}-(m_{\bf B}-m_{\bf{\overline{B}}^{\prime}})^{2}}}=0.8\sim 0.9,

(49)

for the modes we are considering in this work.

Similarly the ratios of exchange and penguin-exchange amplitudes and penguin-annihilation and penguin-exchange factorization amplitudes are

\displaystyle\frac{(E_{\bar{B}_{d}})_{S}}{(PE_{\bar{B}_{d}})_{S}}=0,\quad\frac{(E_{\bar{B}_{d}})_{P}}{(PE_{\bar{B}_{d}})_{P}}\simeq\frac{V_{ub}V^{*}_{ud}}{V_{tb}V^{*}_{td}}\frac{a_{2}}{a_{6}}\frac{(m_{b}+m_{d})m_{u}}{m^{2}_{\bar{B}_{d}}},

(50)

and

\displaystyle\frac{(PA_{\bar{B}_{d}})_{S}}{(PE_{\bar{B}_{d}})_{S}}=0,\quad\frac{(PA_{\bar{B}_{d}})_{P}}{(PE_{\bar{B}_{d}})_{P}}\simeq\frac{a_{5}-a_{3}}{a_{6}}\frac{(m_{b}+m_{d})(m_{u}+m_{d}+m_{s})}{m^{2}_{\bar{B}_{d}}}r_{{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}},

(51)

with
r_{{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}}
defined in Eq. (
31
) and its value given in Table
2
.
For
\Delta S=-1
transition, we have the following expression for ratios of topological factorization amplitudes,

\displaystyle\frac{(A^{\prime}_{B^{-}})_{S}}{(PE^{\prime}_{B^{-}})_{S}}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle-\frac{V_{ub}V^{*}_{us}}{V_{tb}V^{*}_{ts}}\frac{a_{1}}{a_{6}}\frac{(m_{b}+m_{u})(m_{s}-m_{u})}{2m^{2}_{B^{-}}},

\displaystyle\frac{(A^{\prime}_{B^{-}})_{P}}{(PE^{\prime}_{B^{-}})_{P}}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\frac{V_{ub}V^{*}_{us}}{V_{tb}V^{*}_{ts}}\frac{a_{1}}{a_{6}}\frac{(m_{b}+m_{u})(m_{s}+m_{u})}{2m^{2}_{B^{-}}},

(52)

\displaystyle\frac{(E^{\prime}_{\bar{B}_{s}})_{S}}{(PE^{\prime}_{\bar{B}_{s}})_{S}}=0,\quad\frac{(E^{\prime}_{\bar{B}_{s}})_{P}}{(PE^{\prime}_{\bar{B}_{s}})_{P}}\simeq\frac{V_{ub}V^{*}_{us}}{V_{tb}V^{*}_{ts}}\frac{a_{2}}{a_{6}}\frac{(m_{b}+m_{s})m_{u}}{m^{2}_{\bar{B}_{s}}},

(53)

and

\displaystyle\frac{(PA^{\prime}_{\bar{B}_{s}})_{S}}{(PE^{\prime}_{\bar{B}_{s}})_{S}}=0,\quad\frac{(PA^{\prime}_{\bar{B}_{s}})_{P}}{(PE^{\prime}_{\bar{B}_{s}})_{P}}\simeq\frac{a_{5}-a_{3}}{a_{6}}\frac{(m_{b}+m_{s})(m_{u}+m_{d}+m_{s})}{m^{2}_{\bar{B}_{s}}}r_{{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}}.

(54)

Numerically we obtain the following ratios of sizes for topological factorization amplitudes,

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{(A_{B^{-}})_{S}}{(PE_{B^{-}})_{S}}\bigg|

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle 0.0018,\quad\bigg|\frac{(A_{B^{-}})_{P}}{(PE_{B^{-}})_{P}}\bigg|\simeq 0.0048,

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{(E_{\bar{B}_{d}})_{S}}{(PE_{\bar{B}_{d}})_{S}}\bigg|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,\qquad\quad\bigg|\frac{(E_{\bar{B}_{d}})_{P}}{(PE_{\bar{B}_{d}})_{P}}\bigg|\simeq 0.0005,

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{(PA_{\bar{B}_{d}})_{S}}{(PE_{\bar{B}_{d}})_{S}}\bigg|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,\qquad\quad\bigg|\frac{(PA_{\bar{B}_{d}})_{P}}{(PE_{\bar{B}_{d}})_{P}}\bigg|\simeq 0.0012\,|r_{{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}}|,

(55)

for
\Delta S=0
transition, and

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{(A^{\prime}_{B^{-}})_{S}}{(PE^{\prime}_{B^{-}})_{S}}\bigg|

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle 0.0031,\quad\bigg|\frac{(A^{\prime}_{B^{-}})_{P}}{(PE^{\prime}_{B^{-}})_{P}}\bigg|\simeq 0.0032,

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{(E^{\prime}_{\bar{B}_{s}})_{S}}{(PE^{\prime}_{\bar{B}_{s}})_{S}}\bigg|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,\qquad\quad\bigg|\frac{(E^{\prime}_{\bar{B}_{s}})_{P}}{(PE^{\prime}_{\bar{B}_{s}})_{P}}\bigg|\simeq 2.4\times 10^{-5},

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{(PA^{\prime}_{\bar{B}_{s}})_{S}}{(PE^{\prime}_{\bar{B}_{s}})_{S}}\bigg|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,\qquad\quad\bigg|\frac{(PA^{\prime}_{\bar{B}_{s}})_{P}}{(PE^{\prime}_{\bar{B}_{s}})_{P}}\bigg|\simeq 0.0011\,|r_{{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}}|,

(56)

for
\Delta S=-1
transition.
These ratios are very small.

As the
B^{-}_{c}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}
decays are governed by annihilation diagrams, it is useful to have some estimations on the sizes of the annihilation factorization amplitudes.
Using Eqs. (
33
) and (
34
), we have

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{(A^{(\prime)c}_{B^{-}_{c}})_{S,P}}{(A^{(\prime)}_{B^{-}_{u}})_{S,P}}\bigg|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{c}(B_{c})}{p_{c}(B_{u})}\sqrt{\frac{m^{2}_{B_{c}}-(m_{\bf B}\pm m_{\bf{\overline{B}}^{\prime}})^{2}}{m^{2}_{B_{u}}-(m_{\bf B}\pm m_{\bf{\overline{B}}^{\prime}})^{2}}}\frac{f_{B_{c}}f(m^{2}_{B_{c}})}{f_{B_{u}}f(m^{2}_{B_{u}})}\bigg|\frac{V_{cb}}{V_{ub}}\bigg|

(57)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\frac{p_{c}(B_{c})}{p_{c}(B_{u})}\sqrt{\frac{m^{2}_{B_{c}}-(m_{\bf B}\pm m_{\bf{\overline{B}}^{\prime}})^{2}}{m^{2}_{B_{u}}-(m_{\bf B}\pm m_{\bf{\overline{B}}^{\prime}})^{2}}}\times 10.9

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle{\cal O}(10).

Hence, annihilation factorization amplitudes in
B^{-}_{c}
decays are greater than those in
B^{-}_{u}
decays by roughly one order of magnitude.
It should also be noted that although the lifetimes of
B_{u,d,s}
are more or less similar, the lifetime of
B_{c}
is only about one third of their typical lifetime
providing a factor of 3 suppression in
B^{-}_{c}
branching ratios.

As these factorization contributions suffer from severe chiral suppression, the non-factorizable contributions become important and non-negligible.

III.2
Numerical Results on Rates

Predictions on
\bar{B}_{q}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}
and
B^{-}_{c}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}
decay rates will given in this section.
Before we present our result, it will be useful to remind us the detection sensitivities of various baryonic final states.
In Table
4
, we show some final states of the baryons with unsuppressed branching ratios.

final states with all charged states, final states involving
\pi^{0}
or
\gamma
and final states involving
n

Chua:2013zga
;
PDG
.

Predictions on branching ratios of
\overline{B}_{q}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}
decays with inputs using
{\cal B}(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to pp)
and
{\cal B}(B^{-}\to\Lambda\bar{p})
data are given in Tables
5
,
6
,
7
and
8
.
There are four uncertainties, the first one is from the uncertainties of
\chi
and
\kappa
as shown in Eq. (
43
), reflecting the experimental uncertainties in
\overline{B}{}^{0}\to p\bar{p}
and
B^{-}\to\Lambda\bar{p}
rates;
the second one is from varying the penguin strong phase
\phi
, where we use a common strong phase for
P^{(\prime)}
,
P^{(\prime)}_{PE}
and
P^{(\prime)}_{EW}
for simplicity;
the third one is by relaxing the asymptotic relations by varying
r_{t,i}
,
r_{p,i}
,
r_{ewp,i}
and
r_{pe,i}
in Eqs. (
39
) and
(
41
);
the last one is from the uncertainties in sub-leading contributions from
r_{a,i}
,
r_{e.i}
,
r_{pa,i}
,
\eta_{a,i}
,
\eta_{e.i}
and
\eta_{pa,i}
in Eq. (
42
).

Modes are ranked according to decay rates and detectability. Those with three asterisks are the most favorable ones. They have relatively large rates and the baryons can decay to all charged final states. Those with two asterisks are the second ranked ones. They need a
\pi^{0}
or
\gamma
for detection. Those with one asterisk are the third ranked ones, where
\pi^{0}\pi^{0}
,
\pi^{0}\gamma
or
\gamma\gamma
are needed for detection.

As shown in Table
5
,
\overline{B}{}^{0}\to pp
and
B^{-}\to\Lambda\bar{p}
decays are modes ranked as
***
. They have large rates and very good detectability. It is natural that they are the first two modes observed. In fact, they are the only two modes being detected so far.
Note that the second uncertainties of the rates of these two modes from varying the relative phase of
T^{(\prime)}
and
P^{(\prime)}+PE^{(\prime)}
are comparably small.

There are other modes, such as
\overline{B}_{s}^{0}\to\Xi^{-}\overline{\Xi^{-}}
decay, ranked as
***
, which are predicted to have sizable rates and good detectability.
The
\overline{B}_{s}^{0}\to p\bar{p}
rate from factorization contribution is predicted to be very rare, being
10^{-9}
or
10^{-10}
of the
\overline{B}^{0}\to p\bar{p}
or
B^{-}\to\Lambda\bar{p}
decay rate.
The smallness of the factorization contribution to this decay rate can be understood using Eqs.
(
45
) and (
56
).
The
\bar{B}_{s}\to p\bar{p}
rate shown in Table
5
comes mainly from non-factorizable contributions estimated using Eq. (
42
).
Therefore, once the
\bar{B}_{s}\to p\bar{p}
rate is measured, one can use it to give valuable informations on non-factorizable contributions.
We will discuss the consequences of an enhanced
\bar{B}_{s}\to p\bar{p}
decay rate saturating the present experimental bound in next section.

In Table
9
, we show the predictions of
B^{-}_{c}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}
branching ratios. The central values correspond to decay rates from factorization contributions, which are very rare, ranging from
10^{-14}
to
10^{-11}
.
These modes are all governed by annihilation amplitudes
A^{(\prime)c}_{\bf BB^{\prime}}
. Although, as shown in Eq. (
57
), the factorizable
A^{(\prime)c}_{B_{c}}
is about 10 times larger than
A^{(\prime)}_{B_{u}}
, the latter is very small.
Hence, it is natural to have
B^{-}_{c}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}
rates from factorization contributions be suppressed than a typical
B^{-}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}
rate by several orders of magnitude.
Nevertheless with non-factorization contributions, the
B^{-}_{c}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}
branching ratios can be enhanced to
10^{-9}
or even
10^{-8}
level.
Measuring these modes can provide valuable information on non-factorization contributions to annihilation amplitudes.

Note that for
\Delta S=-1
transition, the amplitudes of
\bar{B}_{q}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}
and its conjugated modes are given by

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle V_{ub}V^{*}_{us}|A_{u}|e^{i\delta_{u}}+V_{cb}V^{*}_{cs}|A_{c}|e^{i\delta_{c}},

\displaystyle\bar{A}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle V^{*}_{ub}V_{us}|A_{u}|e^{i\delta_{u}}+V^{*}_{cb}V_{cs}|A_{c}|e^{i\delta_{c}}.

(58)

Since we have
|V_{ub}V^{*}_{us}|/|V_{cb}V^{*}_{cs}|\simeq 0.02
, for

\displaystyle\frac{|A_{u}|}{|A_{c}|}\lesssim{\cal O}(1),

(59)

we should have the following estimation on the direct CP violation
{\mathcal{A}}
,

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{A}}|\simeq 2\left|\frac{V_{ub}V^{*}_{us}}{V_{cb}V^{*}_{cs}}\right|\frac{|A_{u}|}{|A_{c}|}|\sin(\delta_{u}-\delta_{c})|\sin\gamma\lesssim\frac{|A_{u}|}{|A_{c}|}\times 3.7\%.

(60)

Indeed, Eq. (
59
) can be satisfied in the case of pure penguin modes, where we expect

\displaystyle\frac{|A_{u}|}{|A_{c}|}={\cal O}(1),

(61)

and, consequently, from Eq. (
60
), we should have,

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{A}}|\lesssim\frac{|A_{u}|}{|A_{c}|}\times 3.7\%\simeq{\cal O}(1)\times 3.7\%,

(62)

for direct CP violations of
\Delta S=-1
pure penguin modes.
In Table
14
we collect the predictions of direct CP violation of these modes.
We see that the sizes of the predicted direct CP violations agree with the above expectation.

In Table
15
, we collect results of vanishing direct CP violations from pure exchange modes. Since there is no any penguin contribution, the direct CP violations of these modes are predicted to be vanishing. These are null tests of the SM.

In Table
16
, we give the predictions of direct CP violation of
B^{-}_{c}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}
decays. As the decays are from annihilation diagrams, there is no any penguin contribution, and the direct CP violations are all predicted to be vanishing. These are also null tests of the SM.

In Table
17
we compare our results on some of the
\bar{B}_{u,d,s}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}
decay rates to data and other theoretical predictions.
It is encouraging that using
\bar{B}^{0}\to p\bar{p}
and
B^{-}\to\Lambda\bar{p}
rates as inputs, our results satisfy all existing experimental bounds.
This by itself is a non-trivial test.
From the table we see that in the
B^{-}
decay modes, our results agree with those in refs.
Jin:2021onb
;
Cheng:2001tr
, except the
B^{-}\to p\overline{\Delta^{++}}
rate, where the prediction of ref.
Cheng:2001tr
is much larger than ours and exceeds the experimental bound
Belle:2007oni
by one order of magnitude, while the agreement with ref.
Jin:2021onb
on
\bar{B}^{0}\to p\bar{p}
rate rests on the fact that we all use the measured rate as an input.
For the
\bar{B}^{0}
decays, again the agreement with ref.
Jin:2021onb
on
B^{-}\to\Lambda\bar{p}
rate is simply reflecting that we are using the same data as input. The predictions on
B^{-}\to\Lambda\bar{\Lambda}
rate from ref.
Jin:2021onb
and ours are of the same order.
On the other hand our results on the
\bar{B}^{0}
decays differ from those in ref.
Cheng:2001tr
, except for the
\bar{B}^{0}\to\Sigma^{+}\overline{\Delta^{+}}
decay.
For the
\bar{B}^{0}_{s}
decays, our prediction on
\bar{B}^{0}_{s}\to p\bar{p}
rate is below the present experimental bound
LHCb:2022lff
.
Our predictions on
\bar{B}^{0}_{s}\to\Xi^{0}\overline{\Xi^{0}}
and
\bar{B}^{0}_{s}\to\Xi^{-}\overline{\Xi^{-}}
rates are below those from ref.
Jin:2021onb
by roughly one order of magnitude.

Note that the experimental limits on
B^{-}\to\Sigma^{*0}\bar{p}
,
\Lambda\overline{\Delta^{+}}
,
\Delta^{0}\bar{p}
,
p\overline{\Delta^{++}}
,
\bar{B}^{0}\to\Sigma^{*+}\bar{p}

\bar{B}^{0}\to\Lambda\overline{\Delta^{0}}

were reported in 2007
Belle:2007lbz
;
Belle:2007oni
,
while those on
\bar{B}^{0}\to\Delta^{0}\overline{\Delta^{0}}
and
\Delta^{++}\overline{\Delta^{++}}

were reported in 1989
CLEO:1989xsn
.
It will be interesting to see the updated results on these modes.
In particular, as one can see from Table
17
, the experimental upper limit on
B^{-}\to p\overline{\Delta^{++}}
rate reported in ref.
Belle:2007oni

is only a factor of two larger than our predicted rate.
It will be interesting to see the updated search on this mode.

From Eq. (
69
), we see that the amplitude of
\overline{B}_{s}^{0}\to p\bar{p}
decay is given by,

A(\bar{B}^{0}_{s}\to p\overline{p})=-5E^{\prime}_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+E^{\prime}_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-3PA^{\prime}_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}
.
The enhancement in
\overline{B}_{s}^{0}\to p\bar{p}
decay rate can be achieved through the enhancement of
E^{\prime}_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}
,
E^{\prime}_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}
or
PA^{\prime}_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}
.
For illustration, we assume
{\cal B}(\bar{B}^{0}_{s}\to p\bar{p})=0.44\times 10^{-8}
saturating the present experimental bound
LHCb:2022lff

through the enhancement in these topological amplitudes.
Note that
E_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}
,
E_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}
or
PA_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}
will also be enlarged as they are related to
E^{\prime}_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}
,
E^{\prime}_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}
and
PA^{\prime}_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}
, respectively, through CKM factors.
To fit the
\bar{B}^{0}_{s}\to p\bar{p}
rate,
we enhance the non-factorization contributions by adjusting the values of
\eta^{(\prime)}_{E,1}
,
\eta^{(\prime)}_{E,2}
and
\eta^{(\prime)}_{PA}
separately, see Eq. (
42
).
In enhancing
E^{\prime}_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}
,
E^{\prime}_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}
or
PA^{\prime}_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}
, we need

|\eta^{(\prime)}_{E,1}|=7.6
,

|\eta^{(\prime)}_{E,2}|=37.9

or

|\eta^{(\prime)}_{PA}|=3.2
,
respectively.
It seems that enhancing
PA^{\prime}
is the most effective choice.

Given that decay rates of other modes may be affected by the enhancement,
we show in Table
18
branching ratios of
\overline{B}_{q}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}
decays with
E^{(\prime)}_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}
,
E^{(\prime)}_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}
or
PA^{(\prime)}_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}
enlarged. The uncertainties in rates are from the strong phases of these topological amplitudes.
Note that in
\Delta S=0
transition, the
B^{-}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}
and
\bar{B}^{0}_{s}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}
decays are unaffected, while in
\Delta S=-1
transition,
the
B^{-}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}
and
\bar{B}^{0}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}^{\prime}
decays are unaffected as well.
In particular, the
B^{-}\to\Lambda\bar{p}
decay is not affected as it does not have exchange and penguin-annihilation diagrams.
Our finding are as following.
(i) By enlarging
E^{(\prime)}_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}
or
E^{(\prime)}_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}
, we see that the
\bar{B}^{0}_{s}\to p\bar{p}
rate is enlarged, but
\bar{B}^{0}\to p\bar{p}
rate is also enlarged and is in tension with data.
(ii) By enlarging
PA^{(\prime)}_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}
,
\bar{B}^{0}_{s}\to p\bar{p}
rate is enlarged, while the
\bar{B}^{0}\to p\bar{p}
rate agree with data,
and
\bar{B}^{0}_{s}\to\Xi^{0}\overline{\Xi^{0}}
,
\Xi^{-}\overline{\Xi^{-}}

and
\bar{B}_{s}^{0}\to\Lambda\overline{\Lambda}
rates are slightly enlarged.
It seems that enlarging
PA^{\prime}
is a possible way to enhance
\bar{B}_{s}\to p\bar{p}
rate without having significant impacts on other modes.

For
\Delta S=-1
pure penguin modes, the direct CP violation of these modes are predicted to be at most at few percent, see Eqs. (
60
), (
62
) and Table
14
.
It is possible that rare decay modes are sensitive to other effects such as final state interaction (FSI)
FSI
;
Chua:2018ikx
.
Nevertheless, even in the presence of final state interaction the topological amplitude formalism is still applicable
Chiang:2004nm
;
Chua:2018ikx
.
Indeed, it is possible to have final states with charmed flavor to rescatter into charmless final states
FSI
.
These FSI can enhance the charming penguin contribution,
|A_{c}|
,
Ciuchini:1997rj
, giving
|A_{u}|/|A_{c}|<1
, and, consequently, further reduces the sizes of
{\mathcal{A}}
of these pure penguin modes, as one can see by using Eq. (
60
).
Hence
|{\mathcal{A}}|
at most at the level of few %, as shown in Table
14
, is a robust prediction of the SM.

In this work, we study the rates and direct CP violations of
\bar{B}_{u,d,s}\to{{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}}^{\prime}
and
B^{-}_{c}\to{{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}}^{\prime}
decays. We incorporate topological amplitude formalism and the factorization approach. Asymptotic relations at large
m_{b}
are used to simplify decay amplitudes. Using the most up-to-date data on
\bar{B}^{0}\to p\bar{p}
and
B^{-}\to\Lambda\bar{p}
decay rates as inputs, rates and direct CP violations of
\bar{B}_{u,d,s}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}
decays are revised and predicted.
It is interesting that our results satisfy all existing experimental bounds and some predicted rates are close to the bounds.
In particular, the experimental limit on
B^{-}\to p\overline{\Delta^{++}}
rate reported in ref.
Belle:2007oni

is only a factor of two larger than our predicted rate.
It will be interesting to see the updated search on this decay mode.
Factorization diagrams contribute to penguin-exchange, exchange, annihilation and penguin-annihilation amplitudes. Although the resulting penguin-exchange amplitudes are sizable,
the factorization contributions to exchange, annihilation and penguin-annihilation amplitudes suffer from chiral suppression.
Therefore the non-factorizable contributions on these topological amplitudes are important and cannot be ignored.
The factorizable contributions to
\bar{B}_{s}\to p\bar{p}
rate is predicted to be several orders of magnitudes below the present bound, but it can be enhanced by including non-factorizable contributions, as it is governed by exchange and penguin-annihilation diagrams.
The case where the rate can be enhanced through the enhancement on exchange or penguin annihilation amplitudes is discussed.
We find that
by enlarging
E^{(\prime)}_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}
or
E^{(\prime)}_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}
, we see that the
\bar{B}^{0}\to p\bar{p}
rate is also enlarged and is in tension with data.
On the other hand, by enlarging
PA^{(\prime)}_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}
, we see that the
\bar{B}^{0}\to p\bar{p}
rate agree with data,
while
\bar{B}^{0}_{s}\to\Xi^{0}\overline{\Xi^{0}}
,
\Xi^{-}\overline{\Xi^{-}}

and
\bar{B}_{s}^{0}\to\Lambda\overline{\Lambda}
rates are slightly enlarged.
The measurement of
\bar{B}_{s}^{0}\to p\bar{p}
rate can clarify the role of these topological amplitudes and provide valuable information on non-factorization contributions.
The
B^{-}_{c}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}
decays are annihilation modes. Their rates from factorization calculation are found to be very rare but can be enlarged to
10^{-9}
or even
10^{-8}
via non-factorizable contributions.
Small direct CP violations of pure penguin modes in
\Delta S=-1

\bar{B}_{u,d,s}\to{{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}}^{\prime}
decays (see Table
14
) are robust predictions of the SM, while vanishing direct CP violations of exchange modes in
\bar{B}_{u,d,s}\to{{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}}^{\prime}
decays (see Table
15
) and in all
B^{-}_{c}\to{{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}}^{\prime}
decay modes (see Table
16
) are null tests of the SM.
They can be used to test the SM in rare
B_{u,d,s,c}
decays in the baryonic sector.

This work is supported in part by
the Ministry of Science and Technology of R.O.C.
under Grant No MOST-110-2112-M-033 -003.

\overline{B}_{u,d,s,c}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}

{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}
,

\overline{B}
to octet-anti-octet baryonic decays

The full
\bar{B}_{u,d,s,c}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}
decay amplitudes for
\Delta S=0
processes are given by

\displaystyle A(B^{-}\to n\overline{p})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-5P_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-5PE_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+\frac{2}{3}(P_{1EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-P_{3EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+P_{4EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}})-5A_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}},

\displaystyle A(B^{-}\to\Sigma^{0}\overline{\Sigma^{+}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}T_{3{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(5P_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-P_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}})+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(5PE_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-PE_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}})+\frac{1}{3\sqrt{2}}(P_{1EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}

\displaystyle+P_{2EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+2P_{3EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-2P_{4EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}})+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(5A_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-A_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}),

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(5P_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-P_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}})-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(5PE_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-PE_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}})-\frac{1}{3\sqrt{2}}(P_{1EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+P_{2EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}

\displaystyle-4P_{3EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-2P_{4EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}})-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(5A_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-A_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}),

\displaystyle A(B^{-}\to\Sigma^{-}\overline{\Lambda})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}(5P_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+P_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}})-\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}(5PE_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+PE_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}})-\frac{1}{3\sqrt{6}}(P_{1EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-P_{2EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}

\displaystyle-4P_{3EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-2P_{4EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}})-\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}(5A_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+A_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}),

\displaystyle A(B^{-}\to\Xi^{-}\overline{\Xi^{0}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-P_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-PE_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+\frac{1}{3}P_{2EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-A_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}},

\displaystyle A(B^{-}\to\Lambda\overline{\Sigma^{+}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}(T_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-T_{3{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}})-\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}(5P_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+P_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}})-\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}(5PE_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+PE_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{3\sqrt{6}}(5P_{1EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+P_{2EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-4P_{3EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+2P_{4EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}})-\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}(5A_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+A_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}),

\displaystyle A(\bar{B}^{0}\to p\overline{p})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+2T_{4{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+P_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+PE_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+\frac{2}{3}P_{2EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-5E_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+E_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-3PA_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}},

\displaystyle A(\bar{B}^{0}\to n\overline{n})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(T_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+T_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}})-(5P_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-P_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}})-(5PE_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-PE_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}})

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{3}(P_{1EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+P_{2EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-P_{3EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-2P_{4EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}})+E_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-3PA_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}},

\displaystyle A(\bar{B}^{0}\to\Sigma^{+}\overline{\Sigma^{+}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-5E_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+E_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-3PA_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}},

\displaystyle A(\bar{B}^{0}\to\Sigma^{0}\overline{\Sigma^{0}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T_{3{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-\frac{1}{2}(5P_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-P_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}})-\frac{1}{2}(5PE_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-PE_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}})-\frac{1}{6}(P_{1EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}

\displaystyle+P_{2EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+2P_{3EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-2P_{4EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}})-\frac{1}{2}(5E_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-E_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}})-3PA_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}},

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}(T_{3{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+2T_{4{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}})+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{3}}(5P_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+P_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}})+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{3}}(5PE_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+PE_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{6\sqrt{3}}(P_{1EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-P_{2EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+2P_{3EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+10P_{4EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}})

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\sqrt{3}}(5E_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+E_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}),

\displaystyle A(\bar{B}^{0}\to\Sigma^{-}\overline{\Sigma^{-}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(5P_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-P_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}})-(5PE_{1{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-PE_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}})-\frac{1}{3}(P_{1EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+P_{2EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}

\displaystyle-4P_{3EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-2P_{4EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}})-3PA_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}},

\displaystyle A(\bar{B}^{0}\to\Xi^{0}\overline{\Xi^{0}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-3PA_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}},

\displaystyle A(\bar{B}^{0}\to\Xi^{-}\overline{\Xi^{-}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}+PE_{2{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-\frac{1}{3}P_{2EW{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}}-3PA_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}},
