Appendix B
Formulas for decay rates and asymptotic relations for
\langle{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}|(\bar{q}q^{\prime})_{S,P}|0\rangle

The decay
\bar{B}\to{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}},{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}},{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}
and
{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}
decay have the following forms
Jarfi:1990ej

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}\to{\cal B}_{1}\overline{\cal B}_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{u}_{1}(A_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}}+\gamma_{5}B_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}})v_{2},

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}\to{\cal D}_{1}\overline{\cal B}_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\frac{q^{\mu}}{m_{B}}\bar{u}^{\mu}_{1}(A_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}+\gamma_{5}B_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}})v_{2},

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}\to{\cal B}_{1}\overline{\cal D}_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\frac{q^{\mu}}{m_{B}}\bar{u}_{1}(A_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}+\gamma_{5}B_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}})v^{\mu}_{2},

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}\to{\cal D}_{1}\overline{\cal D}_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{u}^{\mu}_{1}(A_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}+\gamma_{5}B_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}})v_{2\mu}+\frac{q^{\mu}q^{\nu}}{m^{2}_{B}}\bar{u}^{\mu}_{1}(C_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}+\gamma_{5}D_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}})v_{2\nu},

(95)

where
q=p_{1}-p_{2}
is the difference of the momenta of the baryons and
u^{\mu},\,v^{\mu}
are the Rarita-Schwinger vector spinors,
Moroi:1995fs

\displaystyle u_{\mu}(\pm\frac{3}{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\mu}(\pm 1)u(\pm\frac{1}{2})

\displaystyle u_{\mu}(\pm\frac{1}{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\epsilon_{\mu}(\pm 1)u(\mp\frac{1}{2})+\sqrt{2}\,\epsilon_{\mu}(0)u(\pm\frac{1}{2}))/\sqrt{3},

(96)

with
\epsilon_{\mu}(\lambda)
the polarization vector.
Using

\displaystyle q\cdot\epsilon(\lambda)_{1,2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mp\,\delta_{\lambda,0}\,m_{B}p_{c}/m_{1,2},

\displaystyle\epsilon^{*}_{1}(0)\cdot\epsilon_{2}(0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m_{B}^{2}-m^{2}_{1}-m^{2}_{2})/2m_{1}m_{2},

(97)

with
p_{c}
the baryon momentum in the center of mass frame
and the fact that
\epsilon^{*}_{1}(0)\cdot\epsilon_{2}(0)

is the largest product among the scalar products of
\epsilon^{*}_{1}(\lambda_{1})
and

\epsilon_{2}(\lambda_{2})
, the last three amplitudes in Eq. (
95
) can be expressed or approximated as

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}\to{\cal D}_{1}\overline{\cal B}_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}\frac{p_{cm}}{m_{1}}\bar{u}_{1}(A_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}}+\gamma_{5}B_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}})v_{2},

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}\to{\cal B}_{1}\overline{\cal D}_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}\frac{p_{cm}}{m_{2}}\bar{u}_{1}(A_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}}+\gamma_{5}B_{{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}})v_{2},

\displaystyle A(\overline{B}\to{\cal D}_{1}\overline{\cal D}_{2})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\frac{m_{B}^{2}}{3m_{1}m_{2}}\bar{u}_{1}(A^{\prime}_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}+\gamma_{5}B^{\prime}_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}})v_{2},

(98)

where

\displaystyle A^{\prime}_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}=A_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}-2(p_{cm}/m_{B})^{2}C_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}},\quad B^{\prime}_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}=B_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}-2(p_{cm}/m_{B})^{2}D_{{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}}.

(99)

Hence decay modes with decuplets (or anti-decuplets) are only in or dominantly in the
\pm\frac{1}{2}
-helicity states.

All
\overline{B}\to{\cal B}\overline{\cal B}
,

{\cal D}\overline{\cal B}
,
{\cal B}\overline{\cal D}
,

{\cal D}\overline{\cal D}
decay amplitudes can be effectively expressed as

A(\overline{B}\to{\mathbf{B}}_{1}\overline{\mathbf{B}}_{2})=\bar{u}_{1}({\bf A}+\gamma_{5}{\bf B})v_{2},

(100)

and it is straightforward to obtain the decay rates giving

\displaystyle\Gamma(\overline{B}\to{\mathbf{B}}_{1}\overline{\mathbf{B}}_{2})=\frac{p_{cm}}{8\pi m_{B}^{2}}[(2m_{B}^{2}-2(m_{{\mathbf{B}}_{1}}+m_{{\mathbf{B}}_{1}})^{2}){\bf A}^{2}+(2m_{B}^{2}-2(m_{{\mathbf{B}}_{1}}-m_{{\mathbf{B}}_{1}})^{2}){\bf B}^{2}].

(101)

We now change to the discussion of finding the asymptotic relations for form factors of scalar and pseudo-scalar density matrix elements,

\langle{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}|(\bar{q}q^{\prime})_{S,P}|0\rangle
.
We follow ref.
Brodsky:1980sx
to obtain the asymptotic relations.
The wave function of a octet or decuplet baryon with helicity
\lambda=-1/2
can be expressed as

|{\mathbf{B}}\,;\downarrow\rangle\sim\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}(|{\mathbf{B}}\,;\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle+|{\mathbf{B}}\,;\downarrow\downarrow\uparrow\rangle+|{\mathbf{B}}\,;\uparrow\downarrow\downarrow\rangle),

(102)

which are composed of 13-, 12- and 23-symmetric terms,
respectively.
For octet baryons,
we have

\displaystyle|p\,;\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\frac{d(1)u(3)+u(1)d(3)}{\sqrt{6}}u(2)-\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}u(1)d(2)u(3)\right]|\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle,

\displaystyle|n\,;\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-|p\,;\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle\,\,{\rm with}\,\,\,u\leftrightarrow d),

\displaystyle|\Sigma^{+}\,;\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-|p\,;\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle\,\,{\rm with}\,\,d\rightarrow s),

\displaystyle|\Sigma^{0}\,;\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg[-\frac{u(1)d(3)+d(1)u(3)}{\sqrt{3}}\,s(2)+\frac{u(2)d(3)+d(2)u(3)}{2\sqrt{3}}\,s(1)

\displaystyle\,\,+\frac{u(1)d(2)+d(1)u(2)}{2\sqrt{3}}\,s(3)\bigg]|\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle,

\displaystyle|\Sigma^{-}\,;\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(|p\,;\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle\,\,{\rm with}\,\,u,d\rightarrow d,s),

\displaystyle|\Lambda\,;\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg[\frac{d(2)u(3)-u(2)d(3)}{2}\,s(1)+\frac{u(1)d(2)-d(1)u(2)}{2}\,s(3)\bigg]|\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle,

\displaystyle|\Xi^{0}\,;\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(|p\,;\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle\,\,{\rm with}\,\,u,d\rightarrow s,u),

\displaystyle|\Xi^{-}\,;\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-|p\,;\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle\,\,{\rm with}\,\,u\rightarrow s),

(103)

and for decuplet baryons, we have

\displaystyle|\Delta^{++};\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u(1)u(2)u(3)|\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle,\qquad\qquad|\Delta^{-};\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle=d(1)d(2)d(3)|\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle,

\displaystyle|\Delta^{+};\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}[u(1)u(2)d(3)+u(1)d(2)u(3)+d(1)u(2)u(3)]|\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle,

\displaystyle|\Delta^{0};\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(|\Delta^{+};\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle\,\,{\rm with}\,\,u\leftrightarrow d),\qquad|\Sigma^{*+};\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle=(|\Delta^{+};\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle\,\,{\rm with}\,\,d\leftrightarrow s),

\displaystyle|\Sigma^{*0};\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{6}}[u(1)d(2)s(3)+{\rm permutation}]|\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle,

\displaystyle|\Omega^{-};\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(|\Delta^{++};\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle\,\,{\rm with}\,\,u\rightarrow s),

(104)

for the
|{\mathbf{B}}\,;\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle
parts.
while the 12- and 23-symmetric parts can be easily obtained by suitable permutation.

From Eq. (
29
), we see that the
\bar{B}_{q^{\prime\prime}}\to{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}
factorization amplitudes are related to the scalar and pseudo-scalar density matrix elements
\langle{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}|(\bar{q}q^{\prime})_{S,P}|0\rangle
.
For example, we have

\displaystyle\langle{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}|(\bar{q}q^{\prime})_{V\mp A}|0\rangle\langle 0|(\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}b)_{V-A}|\bar{B}_{q^{\prime\prime}}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-if_{B_{q^{\prime\prime}}}[(m_{q}-m_{\bar{q}^{\prime}})\langle{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}|(\bar{q}q^{\prime})_{S}|0\rangle

(105)

\displaystyle\quad\hskip 18.0pt\mp(m_{q}+m_{\bar{q}^{\prime}})\langle{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}|(\bar{q}q^{\prime})_{P}|0\rangle].

It is evident that each term in the above matrix element is proportional to light quark masses.
By neglecting higher order contributions from
m_{q}
and
m_{q^{\prime}}
, the quark mass dependence in
\langle{\rm\bf B}\overline{\rm\bf B}^{\prime}|(\bar{q}q^{\prime})_{S,P}|0\rangle
can be ignored.

Following Ref.
Brodsky:1980sx
, we have

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{B}}(p)|{\cal O}|{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}(p^{\prime})\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{u}(p)\left[\frac{1+\gamma_{5}}{2}\,F^{+}(t)+\frac{1-\gamma_{5}}{2}\,F^{-}(t)\right]u(p^{\prime}),

\displaystyle F^{\pm}(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{(\pm)}_{\parallel}({\cal O}:{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}\to{\mathbf{B}})\,F_{\parallel}(t),

(106)

in the large
t
limit.
For simplicity, we illustrate with the space-like case.
Coefficients of
F_{\parallel}
for the

{\mathcal{O}}=\bar{q}_{R}q^{\prime}_{L},\,\bar{q}_{L}q^{\prime}_{R}
cases
are given by

\displaystyle e^{-}_{\parallel}(\bar{q}_{R}q^{\prime}_{L}:{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}\to{\mathbf{B}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 3(\langle{\mathbf{B}};\,\uparrow\uparrow\downarrow|O[q^{\prime}(1,\downarrow)\to q(1,\uparrow)]|{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}\,;\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle

\displaystyle+\langle{\mathbf{B}};\,\downarrow\uparrow\uparrow|O[q^{\prime}(3,\downarrow)\to q(3,\uparrow)]|{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}\,;\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle),

\displaystyle e^{+}_{\parallel}(\bar{q}_{R}q^{\prime}_{L}:{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}\to{\mathbf{B}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,

\displaystyle e^{\pm}_{\parallel}(\bar{q}_{L}q^{\prime}_{R}:{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}\to{\mathbf{B}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{\mp}_{\parallel}(\bar{q}_{R}q^{\prime}_{L}:{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}\to{\mathbf{B}}),

(107)

where the factor
3
in the first line are introduced without lost of generality.
Note that
applying
O[q^{\prime}(1,\downarrow)\to q(1,\uparrow)]
to
|{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime};\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle
changes the
parallel spin
q^{\prime}(1)|\downarrow\rangle
part of

|{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime};\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle

to a
q(1)|\uparrow\rangle
part and likewise for the operation of
O[q^{\prime}(3,\downarrow)\to q(1,\uparrow)]
on
|{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime};\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle
.
It is easy to see that flipping the anti-parallel spin

|\uparrow\rangle
part of

|{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime};\downarrow\uparrow\downarrow\rangle

to
|\downarrow\rangle
will give a helicity
\lambda=-3/2
state,
where the transition amplitude is suppressed.
Hence we only need to consider the parallel spin case.

We can make use of Eq. (
107
) to obtain
e^{\pm}_{\parallel}
for
\langle{\mathbf{B}}(p)|(\bar{q}q^{\prime})_{S,P}|{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}(p^{\prime})\rangle
,

\displaystyle e^{\pm}_{\parallel}((\bar{q}q^{\prime})_{S}:{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}\to{\mathbf{B}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{\pm}_{\parallel}(\bar{q}_{L}q^{\prime}_{R}:{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}\to{\mathbf{B}})+e^{\pm}_{\parallel}(\bar{q}_{R}q^{\prime}_{L}:{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}\to{\mathbf{B}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{-}_{\parallel}(\bar{q}_{R}q^{\prime}_{L}:{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}\to{\mathbf{B}}),

\displaystyle e^{\pm}_{\parallel}((\bar{q}q^{\prime})_{P}:{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}\to{\mathbf{B}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{\pm}_{\parallel}(\bar{q}_{L}q^{\prime}_{R}:{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}\to{\mathbf{B}})-e^{\pm}_{\parallel}(\bar{q}_{R}q^{\prime}_{L}:{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}\to{\mathbf{B}})

(108)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pm e^{-}_{\parallel}(\bar{q}_{R}q^{\prime}_{L}:{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}\to{\mathbf{B}}),

i.e. we have

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{B}}(p)|(\bar{q}q^{\prime})_{S}|{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}(p^{\prime})\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{-}_{\parallel}(\bar{q}_{R}q^{\prime}_{L}:{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}\to{\mathbf{B}})F_{\parallel}(t)\bar{u}(p)u(p^{\prime}),

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{B}}(p)|(\bar{q}q^{\prime})_{P}|{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}(p^{\prime})\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{-}_{\parallel}(\bar{q}_{R}q^{\prime}_{L}:{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}\to{\mathbf{B}})F_{\parallel}(t)\bar{u}(p)\gamma_{5}u(p^{\prime}).

(109)

Therefore, the matrix elements are related with coefficients
e^{-}_{\parallel}(\bar{q}_{R}q^{\prime}_{L}:{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}\to{\mathbf{B}})
.

The matrix elements
\langle{\mathbf{B}}(p)|(\bar{q}q^{\prime})_{S,P}|{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}(p^{\prime})\rangle
occur in topological amplitudes

A^{(\prime)}
,

E^{(\prime)}
,

PE^{(\prime)}
and

PA^{(\prime)}

as shown in Eqs. (
26
) and (
27
), with the help of equations of motion, Eq. (
29
).
By using Eq. (
107
), it is straightforward to obtain the coefficients
e^{-}_{\parallel}
for various matrix elements
\langle{\mathbf{B}}|\bar{q}_{R}q^{\prime}_{L}|{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}\rangle
with results on coefficients
e^{-}_{\parallel}
shown in Tables.
19
,
20
and
21
.
Note that the Clebsch-Gordan coefficients in Eqs. (
26
) and (
27
) canceled out with coefficients
e^{-}_{\parallel}
and the asymptotic relations shown in Eqs. (
33
) and (
34
) are established.
